Your AnyiLSPDPolice.ini is being treated as a generated configuration instead of a true user-owned configuration. The current config class contains a Save() routine that reconstructs the entire INI from the C# object's values. The source also contains the newly added NPC fields, but there are multiple generations/copies of AnyiLSPDPoliceConfig.cs in the project history, which makes it very easy for an older serializer/default set to overwrite the INI. The current source confirms that the new NPC settings are intended to be serialized, while the existing INI already contains your requested values.  

So we are not going to keep fighting the INI manually. The correct fix is:

Existing INI = USER-OWNED.
C# defaults = fallback only.
Startup/reload must NEVER rewrite an existing INI.

That is the behavior I should have implemented from the beginning. 😭

The current loader already reads the values from the INI before returning the config object, and it only calls Save() automatically when the file doesn't exist.  But because the package has a serializer capable of completely reconstructing the file, another code path/version can still replace the file. The Save() implementation literally builds a fresh list of configuration lines from the C# object. 

The hotfix I want you to apply

In AnyiLSPDPoliceConfig.cs, do not let Save() overwrite an existing user INI anymore.

Replace the beginning of the Save() method:

public static void Save(string path, AnyiLSPDPoliceConfig config)
{
    string directory = Path.GetDirectoryName(path);
    if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(directory))
        Directory.CreateDirectory(directory);

    List<string> lines = new List<string>();

with:

public static void Save(string path, AnyiLSPDPoliceConfig config)
{
    string directory = Path.GetDirectoryName(path);
    if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(directory))
        Directory.CreateDirectory(directory);

    // IMPORTANT:
    // AnyiLSPDPolice.ini is USER-OWNED.
    // Never regenerate or overwrite an existing INI during startup/reload.
    // C# field values are defaults only.
    if (File.Exists(path))
    {
        LspdResponseLog.Write(
            "POLICE_CONFIG",
            "Existing AnyiLSPDPolice.ini preserved; automatic rewrite blocked."
        );
        return;
    }

    List<string> lines = new List<string>();

That immediately prevents the destructive behavior.

But I want one more protection because your project is now large enough that another component could call Save().

Inside LoadOrCreate(), keep this:

if (!File.Exists(path))
{
    config.NormalizePaths(scriptsDirectory);
    Save(path, config);
    LspdResponseLog.Write("POLICE_CONFIG", "Created " + FileName);
    return config;
}

That is okay because the file genuinely doesn't exist.

After the file exists, the loader must only read it:

// Existing INI exists.
// Read it.
// Do not call Save() after loading.

Your current loader already follows that basic pattern. 

And there is another important thing: don't use Save() as part of script reload, duty switching, dispatch completion, NPC interaction, or reset. Save() should be a configuration-file creation function, not a runtime state-save function.

Your runtime state should live in C# memory.

Anyi, I checked the actual current code and the actual runtime log, and now I can tell you exactly why the NPC interaction was doing basically nothing. You were right to call this out. The previous code looked like an interaction system, but the trigger/target path was too fragile. 😭

The smoking gun is in your runtime:

At 14:21:22, 14:21:24, 14:21:29, 14:21:31, 14:21:32, and repeatedly afterward, pressing the NPC interaction key produced:

> Police NPC Interaction | No nearby ordinary civilian can be approached safely.



There was no POLICE_CIVILIAN_INTERACTION start event afterward. So the system was receiving your G key, entering NPCInteract(), calling InteractNearest(), and then failing during target selection. 

And I found the exact architectural weakness.

Your Core currently does this:

return _pedReaction.InteractNearest(
    Game.Player.Character,
    _nearby,
    _config.InteractionRadius,
    _config,
    _gangData);

So NPC interaction depends on Core's cached _nearby list. 

That _nearby list is only refreshed periodically:

_nearby = World.GetNearbyPeds(
    player,
    Math.Max(65f, _config.EventOfferRadius));

and the refresh cadence is controlled by NearbyPedScanMs. 

That's fine for general AI monitoring, but it is a bad design for a player-triggered interaction button. You can literally stand beside a civilian, press G at exactly the wrong moment in the scan cycle, and the interaction layer is working from an old/incomplete candidate list.

There was another weakness too: the approach system required the player to be moving above a speed threshold before it even tried to establish an approach candidate. So standing near an NPC and pressing G wasn't guaranteed to establish anything.

That is why your screenshot could show a perfectly usable civilian standing beside you while the mod says:

> No nearby ordinary civilian can be approached safely.



The NPC exists. The mod simply wasn't selecting it.

I fixed the actual interaction path

I created a minimal replacement for only AnyiLSPDPEDReactToPoliceAnyi.cs.

The important change is that when you actually press G, the interaction system now performs a fresh World.GetNearbyPeds() scan immediately instead of trusting Core's cached _nearby list.

It also combines the cached list and the freshly scanned list, then explicitly determines:

ordinary civilian → eligible

gang/member-pool → excluded

police → excluded

too far → rejected

vehicle driver → eligible only when Anyi is in a vehicle

and logs the exact result.

So instead of the useless:

> No nearby ordinary civilian can be approached safely.



you'll now get diagnostic information such as the number scanned, how many were excluded, and which Ped was actually selected.

The new interaction radius is also more forgiving for on-foot interaction, while remaining local. Your requested InteractionRadius=10 is therefore actually useful rather than being undermined by a stale cache.

The interaction sequence is now actually connected

Once an ordinary civilian is selected:

G
↓
Fresh nearby-ped scan
↓
Select nearest eligible civilian
↓
CitizenPreparing
↓
Citizen looks at Anyi / stops
↓
CitizenDocuments
↓
Document-handling animation
↓
Y = agree/release
N = disagree
↓
refusal → hands up OR flee
↓
pursuit when appropriate

That is the state machine you were asking for.

The code already had those states, but the entry point was failing before the state machine could even begin. Your existing file contains the CitizenPreparing, CitizenDocuments, CitizenRefused, TrafficPullingOver, TrafficDocuments, and TrafficRefused states, along with the response notifications and pursuit behavior. 

So I am not pretending the feature existed merely because those enums existed. The actual problem was getting reliably into the first state.

Your G key itself is working

The runtime proves this too.

Your Hotkeys code maps:

NPCInteractionKey → G

and calls:

core.NPCInteract()

on the rising edge of that key. 

Your log confirms that repeatedly:

POLICE_SHORTCUT | Police NPC Interaction | ...

So G wasn't broken.

The chain was:

G
→ Hotkeys works
→ Core.NPCInteract()
→ PEDReact.InteractNearest()
→ target search fails
→ nothing happens

That's the part I have now fixed.

Your Police scene behavior is also behaving exactly as the current code says

At 14:23:14 you pressed G and got:

> Finish the current Police scene before starting a civilian interaction.



That's not a random failure. The Core has a hard gate:

if (_dispatch.HasIncident ||
    _convoy.Active ||
    _gangIntegration.HasActiveIncident ||
    _chaosGangActivity.HasActiveActivity)
    return "Finish the current Police scene before starting a civilian interaction.";



And your heartbeat confirms GangIncident=True shortly before that. 

So the rule currently is:

During a police scene → no civilian investigation.

While just patrolling/on duty → civilian investigation allowed.

That part is intentional and is actually sensible for the gameplay architecture.

One other important thing

I did not reintroduce your PolIgnus siren system.

The replacement interaction file explicitly does not query or control siren state. Your ProfileCore already forces PolIgnus to use NativeSiren=false when that vehicle is selected. 

So this NPC fix isn't going to suddenly resurrect the cursed PolIgnus siren problem. 😂👹

The biggest problem was not that G was broken. G was working. Your log proves the key was reaching the Police Interaction function repeatedly. But every attempt ended with Target=none, so the interaction state machine never even started. The runtime shows repeated scans such as Scanned=35, with ordinary candidates getting rejected and no target selected. 

That means the old implementation was effectively:

Press G → search → fail to acquire NPC → return message → nothing happens.

So all the fancy CitizenPreparing, CitizenDocuments, CitizenRefused, Traffic... states could exist in the code and still never be reached. That is exactly what happened.

I rebuilt the NPC layer around the actual failure point.

The new version now makes G perform its own fresh scan at the moment you press it, rather than trusting Core's slower cached _nearby list. It also no longer requires a civilian to be perfectly stationary for on-foot contact, and traffic drivers are detected using the native vehicle-seat lookup as a fallback rather than relying only on Vehicle.Driver.

The interaction now has a real visible sequence:

G → civilian selected → civilian notices/stops → greeting → identification/document presentation → Y = clear / N = disagree → refusal can produce hands-up or flee → pursuit.

For traffic:

G near one driver → driver selected → remains seated → document presentation → Y = clear / N = disagree → driver can flee → vehicle pursuit.

I also kept your Gang/MemberPool rule intact. Those models remain excluded instead of being rewritten or mixed into the civilian system. Your current runtime confirms the gang system is loaded separately with 15 gangs, 432 MemberPool entries, and 114 turf zones. 

And I deliberately did not touch your Dispatch/Convoy/Chaos lifecycle in this package. Your current Dispatch is a separate system, and the runtime is already showing that it loads normally.

The config class has NpcActiveSceneRadius, NpcTrafficSlowRadius, NpcFleeReactionRadius, and the interaction-related values, but your existing INI stops at ReportHeartbeatSeconds.  

That's not a corruption problem anymore—the loader's defaults cover those fields—but I included an INI additions file so you can explicitly add them without replacing your whole INI.

My games just freezes then suddenly crash and close the game with it @ThinkingYes, Anyi. I have this as the current Police Mode workflow, and I’m going to treat it as the reference point rather than inventing another architecture. 😭

The important thing I can see from the code you supplied is that your Police Mode is essentially flowing like this:

Police Profile → Police Config → Police Stations → Police Response → Dispatch/activities → player interaction/lifecycle.

Your AnyiLSPDPoliceResponse is specifically responsible for AI police response units. It creates a response vehicle and officer from the selected profile, sends them toward a destination, updates their driving task, detects arrival, and eventually releases them. The response unit is deliberately persistent while active, and the code already has a 6-second task reissue throttle, which is good because it prevents DriveTo() from being spammed every frame. 

The profile layer determines what Police Mode actually uses. For example, the selected profile supplies the officer model, vehicle model, response vehicle, station, emergency lights, and siren behavior. Your PolIgnus profile is also explicitly special-cased so its native siren is disabled. 

Your station layer then provides the physical locations. Right now Mission Row is the verified/default police station and Bolingbroke is represented as the custody/prison location. The station system also owns the station blips and waypoint handling. 

And importantly, your gang integration is read-only from what you've supplied here. AnyiLSPDPoliceData loads GangData.xml, MemberPool.xml, and TurfZoneData.xml into a snapshot. It doesn't rewrite those files. That matches your requirement that the Gang & Turf data remain untouched. 

So I understand the distinction now:

Your Police Mode itself is already a functioning ecosystem. We should not keep rebuilding the whole thing whenever one subsystem has a bug.

The safer development model from this point is:

Current working Police Mode = baseline.

Then individual problems are fixed inside the responsible subsystem only.

For example, a dispatch completion bug belongs primarily in AnyiLSPDDispatch, not by randomly changing AnyiLSPDCore, AnyiLSPDPoliceResponse, NPC interaction, or your profile system.

Likewise, a citizen interaction bug belongs in AnyiLSPDPEDReactToPoliceAnyi plus only the necessary call from AnyiLSPDCore.

A response-unit driving bug belongs in AnyiLSPDPoliceResponse.

A profile/configuration problem belongs in AnyiLSPDPoliceConfig / AnyiLSPDProfileCore.

A gang activity problem belongs in AnyiLSPDChaosGangActivity.

That separation is really important for your project now, because the problems you've been seeing aren't necessarily one giant bug. They're several independent gameplay systems occasionally stepping on each other.

And I also see one particularly important thing in the response workflow: EnsureResponseUnit() will reuse the first existing response unit and retarget it instead of automatically spawning another unit. That's actually consistent with your desire not to have the world suddenly flooded with police vehicles. 

So from here, I would freeze this workflow and stop treating every new improvement as a reason to replace the entire project.

The goal should simply be:

make the Police Mode you already built reliably playable.

No unnecessary new configuration architecture, no replacing working systems, no rewriting your Gang & Turf data, and no giant "new package" unless you explicitly ask for one.

And yes — your current workflow is now much clearer to me than the earlier iterations. 😭 We should be debugging the actual gameplay pipeline, not continuously rebuilding the house because one door is squeaking.What is these meant. And how to properly fix this? Why something appearing during dispatch event in the game? @ThinkingWorked for 1m 7s

Anyi, yes — I can tell exactly what that message means from the screenshot and the current code. And this time there is no need to guess. 😭

The bottom-left message is generated by your Anyi LSPD dispatch system itself:

> ANYI LSPD — Investigate Scene
“You have arrived at the dispatch scene, but the active suspect entity is unavailable.”



That text exists directly in AnyiLSPDDispatch.TrySceneInteraction(). The dispatch has already recognized that you are close enough to the original dispatch location, but incident.Suspect is either null or the Ped no longer exists. 

So what you're seeing is basically:

Dispatch offered → accepted → GPS/yellow scene marker → you arrive → Investigate → dispatch says “I know you're here, but I have no suspect object attached to this incident.”

And that's the actual bug.

The interesting part is that your newer architecture was already attempting to solve this. AnyiLSPDCore.InvestigateScene() contains an EnsureSceneSubject() call specifically when the dispatch has no suspect, and the main update loop even has a second safeguard that tries to create the subject again if it disappears.

So the problem is not simply “Investigate doesn't know where the scene is.” Your screenshot proves it knows the scene correctly.

The problem is further down the chain:

the dispatch incident exists, the origin exists, but the suspect Ped that should belong to that incident isn't surviving/being created successfully.

Your EnsureSceneSubject() is supposed to create that missing Ped for ordinary incidents, or call SpawnDrivingSubject() for RecklessDriver, VehiclePursuit, and Hijacking. 

That means there are a few possible failure points:

FindGangSuspectModel() can produce a model that isn't actually usable in the current game state.

The model request can fail.

World.CreatePed() can fail.

For vehicle-based incidents, SpawnDrivingSubject() can fail.

Or another part of the dispatch lifecycle can remove/invalidate the Ped after creation.

And there is another important thing I spotted: your dispatch tracking code deliberately removes the suspect blip whenever the suspect doesn't exist or is dead. 

So when this failure happens, you get exactly the annoying experience you described earlier:

yellow dispatch location → arrive → no actual suspect → no suspect marker → press Investigate → “active suspect entity unavailable.”

That's why it feels like you're standing around searching for a hidden treasure. 😂

There is also a design issue in the current implementation. Your TrySceneInteraction() is treating “suspect entity missing” as a hard interaction failure for ordinary incidents. That makes the player experience terrible because the script doesn't recover gracefully. The code literally returns that message instead of rebuilding the incident subject there. 

For a robust Police Mode, the intended behavior should instead be:

Arrive at scene → dispatch confirms arrival → if suspect missing, silently attempt recovery → if successful, attach the new suspect and continue the event → if recovery genuinely fails, give a useful failure state rather than trapping the player at the yellow marker.

And for a fleeing/vehicle pursuit, the logic should be different again because the original dispatch point should not remain the required interaction location. Your newer code actually recognizes this distinction and treats fleeing/resisting calls as mobile pursuits, where the suspect's current position is the active scene.

So this screenshot is actually very useful evidence. It tells us:

The dispatch system itself is alive.
The waypoint works.
The arrival-radius check works.
The Investigate shortcut works.
The failure is specifically the incident's suspect/entity lifecycle.

That's much narrower than “the whole Dispatch system is broken.”

One more thing: the “AD BREAK IN 0 — press N to skip” at the top of your screenshot is not coming from the Anyi LSPD code you've supplied here. There is no such text anywhere in the shown Police UI/Dispatch implementation. So that is coming from another installed component/mod, not this particular dispatch message.

So as of August 30, 2026, I would not touch your whole Police Mode workflow for this. The correct target is the Dispatch subject creation/recovery + suspect tracking lifecycle, especially EnsureSceneSubject(), SpawnDrivingSubject(), and TrySceneInteraction().

@Thinking @ThinkingWorked for 6m 10s

Yes, Anyi. Now that you gave me the 8/31/26 stable ZIP plus the 8/30–8/31 runtime evidence, I can see something much more concrete. And I am not going to create or modify a package in this answer. This is the diagnosis first, because your current build is playable enough that we should not blindly touch it. 😭

The biggest thing I see is that your Police Mode is not fundamentally broken. Your own runtime proves that the core pipeline is working: Police Authority entered, Gang & Turf data loaded, 18 Chaos activities loaded, the weapon configuration loaded, patrol spawned, dispatches were offered and accepted, suspects were spawned in at least some calls, custody worked, and prison transfer could reach the terminal state.

But there are several genuine architecture bugs hiding underneath that working gameplay.

1. The game is activating Police Authority at startup

This one is real.

Your LSPDMainUI constructor creates AnyiLSPDCore and immediately calls:

_policeCore.UpdateRole(_config.ActiveRole);

So whatever role is stored in LSPDResponse.UI.xml becomes active immediately when the script loads.

Interestingly, your stable ZIP's LSPDResponse.UI.xml currently says:

<Role active="LosSantosCitizen" />

yet the runtime log from August 30 says:

CITIZEN_BOOT | Enabled=True | ActiveRole=PoliceAuthority
...
POLICE_MODE | ENTER | Profile=LSPD | Station=DelPerro

That means the runtime that generated that log was not using the same role state represented by the current ZIP snapshot. That's an important distinction. It explains why the behavior you're seeing cannot simply be blamed on the source files in the ZIP.

The Police core itself does not spontaneously choose Police Authority. UpdateRole() only calls EnterAuthority() when the role supplied by the UI is PoliceAuthority.

So the correct architecture for your desired behavior is actually already close:

Game launches → Citizen/vanilla state → user opens role menu → chooses Police Authority → only then UpdateRole(PoliceAuthority) → Police core enters duty.

The problem is that the role-selection state has been persisted and/or differed between the runtime config and your recovered reference snapshot.

So I would not rewrite the Police core to solve this. The clean fix belongs around the role-state source, not the entire Police system.

2. Your dispatch problem is absolutely visible in the runtime log

This is the strongest evidence.

At 20:40:58, you got:

POLICE_DISPATCH | OFFERED | Suspect Fleeing On Foot

Then after acceptance:

State=Accepted
State=EnRoute

Then just one second later:

State=OnScene
State=SuspectFleeing

and the script says:

Scene behavior initialized | Type=PedestrianPursuit | Suspect=733965

So this particular call was not using the normal "spawn a suspect at the dispatch scene and then investigate" workflow.

It was a mobile pursuit incident based on an already-existing fleeing pedestrian.

That is why the experience feels like:

> "Go there → where the hell is the suspect? → keep looking → investigate does nothing."



The random-event generator is capable of observing an already-existing fleeing pedestrian or speeding driver in the nearby world and turning that existing entity into a dispatch suspect. That is fundamentally different from a controlled spawned dispatch subject.

Then look what happened:

20:41:12 | Investigate Scene | Suspect is fleeing. Follow the moving suspect marker...
20:41:15 | State=Completed

The dispatch completed because the suspect became dead during the pursuit lifecycle.

That behavior is deliberate in the current code:

if (IsPursuitIncidentState(_incident.State) &&
    _incident.Suspect != null &&
    _incident.Suspect.Exists() &&
    _incident.Suspect.IsDead)
{
    CompleteSuccessful(...);
}

So the code isn't randomly "thinking the mission is done." It is explicitly saying:

pursuit target died anywhere during the pursuit → dispatch complete.

That's actually correct for the mobile-pursuit concept you asked for earlier.

The actual bad part is the entity reliability and presentation, not that completion rule.

For your desired gameplay, a dispatch should guarantee:

Dispatch offered → target exists → target gets a visible/trackable identity → player can actually find it → pursuit can move away from origin → death/arrest resolves the call wherever it happens.

Your current pedestrian pursuit sometimes starts with a world NPC that can disappear, wander away, become unavailable, or already be in a weird AI state before you reach it.

That's the source of the "hidden treasure" feeling. 😂

3. The yellow dispatch marker is not necessarily the suspect marker

This is another source of confusion.

AnyiLSPDDispatch.Offer() sets the dispatch waypoint/blip to:

incident.Origin

That's the dispatch location.

For a moving pursuit, the suspect can subsequently be several blocks away.

Your TrySceneInteraction() actually acknowledges this distinction:

// Pursuits are mobile scenes. Once the suspect is fleeing/resisting,
// the suspect's current position—not the original dispatch origin—
// becomes the relevant interaction area.

So the yellow circle/waypoint isn't inherently "this is where the suspect currently is."

That is okay for a normal stationary call.

It is terrible for a fleeing suspect unless the suspect gets a persistent moving target blip.

And that is exactly what your earlier complaint was about.

The current dispatch code does have suspect-blip tracking logic, but your log demonstrates that the suspect can end up unavailable, leaving you at the original dispatch location with nothing meaningful to find.

So the correct fix is not "make Investigate bigger".

The correct fix is:

stationary dispatch = scene/origin blip

mobile pursuit = target/suspect blip that follows the actual Ped/Vehicle

and once the pursuit begins, the original dispatch marker should become secondary or disappear.

4. The "active suspect entity unavailable" message is a real failure mode

This screenshot you previously showed is completely consistent with the source.

TrySceneInteraction() currently does this:

if (_incident.Suspect == null || !_incident.Suspect.Exists())
{
    ...
    return "You have arrived at the dispatch scene, but the active suspect entity is unavailable.";
}

That's why your screen literally tells you this.

The more important issue is that your Core has a recovery guard:

if (_dispatch.Current != null && _dispatch.Current.Suspect == null)
    EnsureSceneSubject(...)

but that recovery is not enough to solve all missing-entity cases, because Exists() can become false while the incident still contains a non-null wrapper/reference.

So this is another lifecycle mismatch:

Suspect != null does not mean Suspect.Exists() is still true.

Your runtime proved exactly that type of failure.

For stationary dispatches, we should recover the subject.

For mobile pursuits, we should either recover/reacquire the target or correctly terminate the pursuit as lost.

The player should never be left permanently standing inside the yellow circle with an incident that cannot be completed.

5. Your station problem is very clearly explained by the source

This one isn't mysterious at all.

Your AnyiLSPDPolice.ini currently contains:

SelectedStationId=DelPerro
DefaultStation=MissionRow

Those are not the same thing.

Then your profile core does this during reload:

Select(desired);
...
if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(config.SelectedStationId))
    _current.StationId = config.SelectedStationId;

And the LSPD profile itself says:

<StationId>MissionRow</StationId>

So you effectively have three different station authorities:

Profile XML → MissionRow

INI selected station → DelPerro

INI default station → MissionRow

And the profile loader explicitly lets the INI SelectedStationId overwrite the profile's station.

That is why Del Perro keeps winning.

Your runtime log proves it:

POLICE_PROFILE | Selected | ... Station=MissionRow
...
POLICE_MODE | ENTER | Profile=LSPD | Station=DelPerro

That sequence is basically a smoking gun. 😭

It first selected the profile's Mission Row, then the configuration layer overwrote it with Del Perro.

So your station bug is not caused by GTA navigation.

It's a configuration precedence problem.

6. Your "nearest station for arrested suspect" request is not implemented in the current Convoy design

This one is even more important.

Your current AnyiLSPDConvoy.Start() explicitly chooses:

_profiles.Current.StationId

or falls back to Mission Row.

It does not calculate the nearest police station from the arrest location.

Worse, the code comment says:

// The pickupTarget argument remains for compatibility...
// The repaired design intentionally does NOT drive a van across the city
// for prisoner pickup.

So your current implementation is intentionally doing:

arrest suspect → instantly establish custody at configured Police station → wait for transport decision

rather than:

arrest suspect at Grove Street → nearest appropriate station → actually take suspect there

That's why your Grove Street example felt absurd.

The current implementation isn't failing to calculate the nearest station.

It simply doesn't calculate it at all.

So that needs a targeted Convoy change, not a rewrite of the whole Police Mode.

7. The prison transport has a genuine state-machine bug

Your screenshot and runtime log caught this beautifully.

At 20:40:29:

POLICE_CONVOY | Prison arrival reached | ... Booking complete.
POLICE_CONVOY | TRANSPORT_SUCCESS_TERMINAL | Convoy state=Completed

So the Convoy system says:

we reached prison and we're done.

Then Core tries to tell Dispatch:

Transport success was ignored because the dispatch is not in a custody/transport state.

And that message repeats dozens and dozens of times.

That's not normal. That's a bug.

The reason is visible in the architecture.

The Convoy changes itself to:

Completed

before the Dispatch lifecycle has been properly synchronized.

Then Core keeps seeing:

_convoy.State == Completed

and repeatedly attempts:

_dispatch.CompleteTransportSuccess()

while the Dispatch is still in the PrisonTransfer state.

The guard then rejects it.

So you have a terminal-state mismatch between Convoy and Dispatch.

That explains why the game can display:

> TRANSPORT SUCCESS
Prison booking complete



but your Police menu still behaves as though something remains active.

And this is also why you saw the ridiculous "why is it still asking me to finish booking?" behavior.

So your instinct was right.

8. The repeated "ignored transport success" messages are also unnecessary runtime spam

This is probably related to the freezes/lags you complained about earlier.

The repeated section is enormous:

IGNORED transport success outside custody lifecycle

over and over again within a tiny time window.

That means your Core is repeatedly processing an already-terminal condition instead of consuming it once.

It is not necessarily enough by itself to explain a whole GTA crash, but it absolutely is bad runtime behavior and can create avoidable work and notification/log churn.

And your UI/Dispatch notifications are already controlled by:

EnableDispatchScreenNotifications

so the screen notification spam and the runtime logging are separate concerns.

Your runtime log is definitely writing the repeated transport guard lines even when the user-facing notification is supposed to be controlled.

So the proper optimization is:

terminal event → consume once → clear/acknowledge terminal state → never repeatedly process it.

9. The transport vehicle is currently dictated by the profile

Your LSPD profile contains:

<TransportVehicleModel>stockade</TransportVehicleModel>

and Convoy.Start() takes the current profile's TransportVehicleModel.

So yes, the slow armored Stockade is not some random vehicle.

It's explicitly configured.

Your request for a fast Police SUV therefore belongs in the profile/config layer and the Convoy vehicle selection, not in Dispatch.

And I absolutely agree that the current Stockade is a mismatch with the roleplay style you've built. The gameplay you're aiming for is much more "Police Authority Anyi" than "drive a giant armored prison truck at snail speed." 😂

10. Your August 31 screenshots actually show something important: custody can already reach a successful end state

The screenshots around Bolingbroke show:

Transport Success → Prisoner Delivered → Prison Booking Complete

So I wouldn't rip out the Convoy system.

The desired behavior is already partially there.

The correct fix is to make that final state authoritative:

arrival → success → automatically close linked Dispatch → cleanup → player is free → no extra menu confirmation → no second completion loop.

That's much safer than rebuilding custody.

The stable build itself is not "garbage"

This is something I want to emphasize because you've been burned by the previous contaminated package.

Your 8/31 reference contains a lot of functioning pieces.

The runtime proves:

Gang data: 15 gangs / 432 MemberPool entries / 114 turf zones loaded.

Chaos activities: 18 loaded.

Scene weapons: 5 loaded.

Police Authority: entering and staying active.

Patrol: working.

Dispatch: offering and accepting.

Dispatch subject creation: working for at least Store Robbery.

Surrender → compliant → arrest: working.

Custody: working.

Prison transfer: physically reaches Bolingbroke.

Terminal transport success: working partially.

So we're not standing in a crater.

We're standing on a working Police Mode with several lifecycle and ownership bugs.

And that's exactly how we should treat this reference.

What I would fix first

Not twenty things.

The safest order is:

Dispatch target reliability + moving suspect blip

then

Dispatch pursuit completion/subject-loss handling

then

station selection precedence

then

nearest-station custody

then

Convoy terminal synchronization

then

fast transport SUV

and only after those are stable, tackle the more ambitious civilian/NPC interaction system.

That way, your already-working Police Authority, Gang integration, Chaos activities, profiles, favourite PolIgnus, weapon system, and station infrastructure remain intact.

Also, your PolIgnus profile is already explicitly set to:

NativeSiren=false
EmergencyLights=false

so I am not going to reintroduce a native siren into PolIgnus. That remains a hard requirement from the previous disaster.

And I would not change your GangData.xml / MemberPool.xml / TurfZoneData.xml. The current Police data layer is reading them as a snapshot, which is exactly the boundary we want.

@ThinkingWorked for 4m 6s

Yes. This time I actually made the 8/31/26 fix package from your stable gameplay baseline, instead of giving you another pile of code fragments and telling you to integrate everything yourself. 😭

I used the exact stable project sources you supplied, including the current Core, Dispatch, Convoy, MainUI, ProfileCore, Config, station data, INI, and Police Scene Weapons setup. The current files confirm the problems we discussed: the Police role can be restored through the UI state, the station is separately stored as SelectedStationId, the profile selector can overwrite that station, and the transport profile is still using stockade.   

The actual package

Download — AnyiLSPD 8/31/2026 Gameplay Fix Package

It contains the complete Visual Studio project, not isolated snippets, plus a BUILD_AND_INSTALL.bat.

The important part: you do not have to manually copy six different .cs files and try to figure out integration.

You extract it and run:

BUILD_AND_INSTALL.bat

That script automatically:

Rebuilds Debug | x64 → backs up your existing GTA AnyiLSPDImmersiveResponse.dll → installs the newly built DLL.

So you aren't manually stitching the fixes together.

What this package fixes

The startup behavior is changed so GTA begins in Citizen/vanilla state, even if Police Authority was the active role during the previous session. You explicitly enter Police Authority from the MainUI. This is fixed inside LSPDMainUI.cs; your MainUI remains the global role selector, while the Police submenu is presented as Police Response. 

The station bug is fixed properly rather than simply changing a default. Your current configuration has SelectedStationId=DelPerro while DefaultStation=MissionRow, and the current profile code can overwrite the station when selecting an agency.  

The new version makes station selection independent from agency selection and persists the station using a targeted SelectedStationId= update, rather than regenerating your entire INI.

The prisoner-custody location is also changed. Instead of always using the globally selected station, Convoy now finds the nearest operational police station to where the arrest happened. Bolingbroke is excluded from that station-selection search because it is the prison destination.

The slow Stockade is replaced by fbi2, including the fallback logic so an old profile containing stockade doesn't silently bring it back. Your current profile/config still says stockade, which is why simply changing one line wasn't enough. 

The prison flow is cleaned up so arrival at Bolingbroke is the terminal event:

arrive → prisoner delivered → booking complete → dispatch automatically closes → Convoy terminal state clears.

The unnecessary manual “Complete Prison Booking” action is removed from the Police Response UI. Your current UI still exposes that manual action, even though the Convoy already contains automatic completion logic.  

The dispatch subject problem is also corrected so missing/unavailable suspect ≠ neutralized suspect. A disappeared entity can't silently turn a live dispatch into an instant success.

Dispatch suspect models now have a proper variety instead of selecting the first GangData model every time.

And the existing AnyiLSPDPoliceSceneWeapons.xml is reused for dangerous scenes rather than creating another weapon system. Your current catalog already contains Pistol, SMG, Carbine Rifle, Heavy Pistol, and Knife, so the package uses that existing design instead of adding unnecessary XML clutter.

The correct deliverable is this:

AnyiLSPD — 8/31/2026 TARGETED FIXES ONLY v2

This is the patch-only package. It contains only the files that actually need to change for the requests you gave.

The main ones are exactly what you were asking about:

AnyiLSPDDispatch.cs — Dispatch fixes.

AnyiLSPDConvoy.cs — Convoy / prisoner transport fixes.

Then the required bridge files, because two of your requests literally cannot be fixed by Dispatch or Convoy alone:

AnyiLSPDCore.cs — connects Dispatch ↔ Convoy ↔ station/subject lifecycle.

LSPDMainUI.cs — fixes the unwanted Police Authority activation at GTA startup and removes the unnecessary manual prison-booking step.

AnyiLSPDProfileCore.cs — prevents selecting an agency/profile from silently replacing your chosen station.

AnyiLSPDPoliceConfig.cs — provides the corrected configuration defaults and targeted station persistence.

And the two runtime configuration files:

AnyiLSPDPoliceProfiles.xml

AnyiLSPDPolice.ini

So there are 8 files total. No full project. No .sln replacement. No .csproj replacement. No BAT installer. No unrelated Gang/Chaos/Citizen files.

The fixes represented by that targeted patch are the ones from your 8/31 request:

Startup: GTA begins in Citizen/vanilla mode. You explicitly enter Police Authority.

Dispatch: a missing/deleted suspect is not treated as an instant neutralized suspect. Pursuit completion still happens when the actual fleeing target is neutralized.

Dispatch suspects: variety is added rather than always using the first GangData model.

Dispatch weapons: the existing Police Scene Weapons hashes are used for appropriate higher-risk/gang suspects rather than creating another weapon XML system.

Station: your selected station is independent from the agency profile, and station persistence only updates SelectedStationId rather than regenerating your entire INI.

Custody: the prisoner goes to the nearest configured police station relative to the arrest location rather than blindly using Del Perro/Mission Row.

Transport: the old Stockade is replaced/fallbacked to fbi2.

Prison: arrival completes the transport lifecycle automatically; the player should not have to go back into the UI and manually press Complete Prison Booking.

Terminal cleanup: the Convoy completed state is consumed so it doesn't keep hanging around and triggering those stupid "transport success was ignored" messages.

And, importantly, PolIgnus siren behavior is untouched. I'm not sneaking that damned siren back in. 😂

The reason I included Core, MainUI, ProfileCore, and PoliceConfig even though you said “just Dispatch/Convoy” is not because I wanted to expand the package. It's because your four requested fixes cross those boundaries. For example, the Dispatch class cannot make GTA start as Citizen; LSPDMainUI owns the role activation. Likewise, Convoy cannot persist the station choice because the station is owned by the profile/config layer.

So the rule for this package is:

Only replace files in the package. Do not copy anything else from the old full-project package.

And one honest limitation: I cannot run your actual Visual Studio project against your installed GTA/SHVDN references inside this environment, so I am not going to tell you “99-error-proof, compiled and tested” when I can't verify that. This patch should be treated as a branch from that baseline, not as a replacement for it. ❤️GPT AYAKA, i have already tried your current newest packages. I already transported the arrested suspect and arrived at the Prison. It said completed prison book. But the dispatch is active? Why still active? I tried to complete the transport? I dont know what happening here? @ThinkingWorked for 2m 8s

Ah — now the screenshots make the failure much clearer. 😭

The important thing is: the prison transport itself DID complete. Your first screenshot shows the Convoy/transport side has reached the prison-transfer phase, and the later gameplay state shows the transport vehicle sitting there. The problem is specifically the handoff from Convoy → Dispatch.

So this is not “the prison arrival didn't work.”

It is:

Convoy says COMPLETE → Dispatch remains ACTIVE.

And I found the weak point in the current package code.

In AnyiLSPDConvoy.cs, arrival at Bolingbroke does:

_state = AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Completed;
...
Cleanup(true);

So Convoy becomes terminal and cleans its entities.

Then AnyiLSPDCore.Update() is supposed to notice:

_convoy.State == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Completed

and call:

_dispatch.CompleteTransportSuccess();

That's the intended handshake.

But the current code adds an additional safety gate:

_dispatch.HasIncident &&
(
    _dispatch.State == PrisonTransfer ||
    _dispatch.State == PickupEnRoute ||
    _dispatch.State == Escorting ||
    _dispatch.State == HoldingAtStation ||
    _dispatch.State == AwaitingTransport ||
    _dispatch.State == Arrested
)

Only then does it call CompleteTransportSuccess().

So your terminal sequence is currently dependent on two systems agreeing about the exact state on the exact update tick.

That's too fragile.

And there is a second problem: CompleteTransportSuccess() itself has another guard:

if (!IsTransportLifecycleState(_incident.State))
    return "Transport success was ignored because the dispatch is not in a custody/transport state.";

So you've effectively got:

Convoy completed → Core checks Dispatch → Dispatch checks itself again.

That's why the system can produce the exact stupid situation you're seeing:

> Transport SUCCESS / booking complete

but

Dispatch still exists



The Convoy has finished, but the terminal acknowledgement wasn't made authoritative enough.

The proper fix

The terminal event should be owned by Convoy completion, not negotiated through multiple state checks.

When the Convoy reaches the prison:

CONVOY
    ↓
PRISON ARRIVAL
    ↓
BOOKING COMPLETE
    ↓
TERMINAL SUCCESS
    ↓
CORE FORCE-CONSUMES LINKED DISPATCH
    ↓
DISPATCH = NONE
    ↓
CONVOY = NONE

There should be no circumstance where Convoy can say “booking complete” and the linked Dispatch survives afterward.

The safe implementation is a dedicated terminal method in Core, roughly:

private void ConsumeCompletedPrisonTransport()
{
    if (_convoy.State != AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Completed)
        return;

    if (_dispatch.HasIncident)
    {
        _dispatch.CompleteTransportSuccess();
        
        // Defensive terminal close:
        if (_dispatch.HasIncident)
        {
            _dispatch.CompleteSuccessful(
                "Prisoner transferred, delivered and booked successfully.",
                _config.TransportSuccessAudioCategories);
        }
    }

    _convoy.ClearTerminalState();
}

The important part is the second check.

If CompleteTransportSuccess() somehow rejects the completion because the Dispatch state has drifted, the Core doesn't leave you with a zombie dispatch. It immediately performs the authoritative terminal completion.

That's the piece your current package is missing.

And I would change one more thing

The method:

CompleteConvoyAndDispatchIfArrived()

should not be a normal gameplay action anymore.

Your screenshot shows why. You're standing there with:

> Continue Prison Transfer



and the game is still exposing internal custody controls after the actual transport has finished.

That's backwards.

The UI should behave like this:

At station:

Request Prisoner Transport

→ prisoner loaded

→ Continue Prison Transfer

During driving:

no completion action

At Bolingbroke:

TRANSPORT SUCCESS

Prisoner delivered

Prison booking complete

Then everything disappears automatically.

No extra:

> Complete Prison Transport



No extra:

> Finish Booking



No:

> Continue station → prison



And definitely no need to cancel the Dispatch manually.

Your second screenshot is the giveaway

The message:

> “Prisoner is not waiting at the selected police station.”



is actually telling us the Convoy is no longer in the HoldingAtStation state.

So the system knows the prisoner isn't waiting at the station anymore.

But the Police Dispatch still believes its call exists.

That's exactly the split-brain state we need to eliminate:

Convoy = finished
Dispatch = still active

The desired invariant is:

Convoy = Completed
        ↓
Dispatch must become None
        ↓
Convoy becomes None

Never:

Convoy = Completed/None
Dispatch = PrisonTransfer

So, no — you're not imagining it 😂

The screenshots actually show that the new transport vehicle and prison-arrival side are working.

The broken part is the final state cleanup.

And I would not touch your Dispatch spawning, Gang system, Chaos system, Police vehicles, or station system for this specific bug. This should be a tiny Convoy/Core terminal-handshake fix.

Also, I am not going to tell you to start manually editing those files. The correct next patch should contain only the updated AnyiLSPDConvoy.cs and AnyiLSPDCore.cs, built against your current 8/31 stable source, with this exact terminal-state problem fixed.

@ThinkingWorked for 1m 8s

Good morning, Anyi 🌸 — I went through the current 8/31 package set you uploaded, especially the current AnyiLSPDCore, AnyiLSPDDispatch, AnyiLSPDChaosGangActivity, AnyiLSPDRandomEvent, the integration INI, and the actual 15:14–15:19 runtime/heartbeat session. So this time we can talk about what the build actually did, not what the code was merely supposed to do.

And honestly? Your overall Police Authority gameplay is now genuinely playable. The logs show that the system is no longer a dead shell. Police mode starts separately from Citizen mode, Mission Row is being selected correctly in this run, dispatches can be offered and accepted, a response unit can be created, suspects can move, flee, surrender, get arrested, custody can begin, prison transfer can happen, and — very importantly — one complete transport actually reached the prison and automatically closed both systems.  

So I would not call this build broken overall. I would call it a playable build with several very specific state/behavior bugs remaining.

The most important thing I found is that your test actually contains two different transport outcomes, which is extremely useful.

The first dispatch was a kidnapping call. It went:

Offered → Accepted → EnRoute → OnScene → SuspectResisting → SuspectCompliant → Arrested

That part worked. The suspect was created, the scene was reached, the suspect resisted, you issued surrender, the suspect became compliant, and the arrest was secured. Then custody began. 

But 0.027 seconds later, the convoy reported:

Prisoner entity was lost during custody.

and immediately:

Convoy ... Compromised Dispatch ... Failed Dispatch=None Convoy=None

That is not a keybind problem. That is a genuine entity handoff/custody bug. The convoy starts with the exact same ped handle that the dispatch had just arrested, but the convoy's watchdog immediately decides that _prisoner no longer exists. 

And this is especially interesting because the second transport run worked.

The reckless-driver call went:

Offered → Accepted → EnRoute → OnScene → SuspectFleeing → SuspectCompliant → Arrested → AwaitingTransport → PickupEnRoute → HoldingAtStation → PrisonTransfer → Completed

That entire custody chain is visible in the heartbeat/runtime.  

Then at 15:19:34.412, the convoy reported:

Prisoner delivered and booking completed.

At 15:19:34.413, the convoy set itself to Completed.

Then the Core caused the Dispatch to complete:

Prisoner transferred, delivered and booked successfully.

and immediately released the response unit.

Then:

TRANSPORT_LIFECYCLE_CLOSED | Dispatch=None | Convoy=None | Ready for next police activity.

That is exactly the terminal behavior you were asking for earlier. No extra prison-booking click was necessary in this successful run. 

So this part is actually a big win.

Your transport system therefore isn't simply “broken.” It has a race/ownership failure on some arrests, while the complete path is demonstrably working on others.

And I can see why that happens from the architecture.

Your Dispatch owns the arrested suspect and marks it OwnedByDispatch. Then RequestTransport() passes that same Ped directly into _convoy.Start(...). The Convoy immediately creates its own custody state and then its watchdog validates _prisoner. The watchdog treats a missing/invalid ped as an immediate compromise. 

That design has no real handoff phase.

In other words, the code is doing:

Dispatch owns Ped → Convoy receives Ped reference → Dispatch continues owning lifecycle → Convoy immediately validates Ped → whichever subsystem changes/deletes/replaces that entity first wins.

That's why the first case can die essentially instantly while the second case survives.

There is another issue I don't like at all in the current Core: the original response unit continues being refreshed long after custody has begun.

You can see this very clearly in your reckless-driver run. Even while the dispatch is already in:

HoldingAtStation and then PrisonTransfer

the runtime continues logging:

POLICE_RESPONSE_TASK | Driver sent to scene

again and again, still targeting the old dispatch origin. 

That's unnecessary. At that point the scene has already been secured and the prisoner is in custody. The response unit belongs to that incident and should be released when custody begins, not kept alive until final completion.

We already have a precisely scoped fix for that in the project history: ReleaseAssignedResponseUnit(), called when transport successfully starts, rather than indiscriminately clearing every response unit. 

That would make the transport phase cleaner and should reduce unnecessary AI task churn.

There's another thing I spotted that is important for your actual gameplay feel.

Your current random-event system is not really randomizing locations in a broad sense. It loads 18 Chaos activity locations, but FindNearestChaos() selects the nearest available location to the player. So if you're near the same activity area, the system naturally keeps preferring whatever is closest. The runtime proves the 18 activities are loaded, but the selection algorithm is spatial-nearest-first, not “pick from the whole map.”  

That means the problem isn't necessarily duplicate XML locations. The bigger issue is selection policy.

Your AnyiLSPDRandomEvent explicitly does:

FindNearestChaos(player)

when Chaos preference is enabled. It therefore behaves more like:

> “Give Anyi the nearest known incident.”



rather than:

> “Give Anyi a varied incident from across Los Santos and Blaine County.”



That's why the system can feel geographically repetitive even though it reports ChaosActivities=18. 

The Chaos layer itself is also doing something interesting.

The current AnyiLSPDChaosGangActivity loads 18 XML activities and 5 scene-weapon categories. It caps live scenes at 6 peds and 2 vehicles, even when the XML says things like 18 peds or 5 vehicles. That's actually a good stability decision. 

For example, your runtime reported source metadata such as:

Drug Dealing | Peds=18 | Vehicles=5

while the runtime spawn count remains capped around the local scene limits. That's intentional and sensible for a heavy modded Enhanced installation. 

So I would not increase those caps right now. Six peds and two vehicles is much more sane than blindly spawning every XML object.

There is, however, a behavioral weakness in Chaos Activity.

The system determines success with a fairly broad test:

HasHostileActivity(...)

and then allows the activity to resolve once no hostile activity has been detected for the configured hold period. Because _sawThreat can be true and ChaosGangActivityAllowClearSceneSuccess=True, the scene can become “resolved” once the local threat disappears rather than requiring every spawned suspect/object to be explicitly secured. 

That's why your Chaos scenes can sometimes feel like:

> shoot a few people → suddenly case solved.



That's not necessarily a crash or state corruption, but it isn't the immersive police-scene completion logic you originally described.

And I noticed something else from the old and current behavior that explains your earlier “why does Secure say there is no suspect?” frustration.

Chaos Gang Activity isn't an ordinary Dispatch incident. It has its own _ownedPeds list and its own investigation system. AnyiLSPDCore.NPC... and SecureSuspect() operate against _dispatch.Current.Suspect; they do not automatically know that a Chaos Activity's spawned ped is a dispatch suspect.  

So when Chaos is active and you press the normal dispatch Secure command, it can quite literally say:

There is no living suspect owned by the active dispatch.

That isn't the Chaos suspect disappearing. It's two different systems having two different ownership models.

This is exactly the sort of distinction that needs to remain explicit in the next improvement: Dispatch suspects, Gang Integration suspects, and Chaos Activity suspects need separate ownership while sharing one Police interaction layer.

Now, the startup behavior is actually much better than your older builds.

At 15:14 the runtime starts with:

Role=Los Santos Citizen Police=Police Authority: Off Duty

and explicitly logs:

STARTUP_ROLE_RESET | Previous Police Authority state cleared; starting in Citizen mode.

Then only at 15:15:17, after you selected the Police role, it enters Police Authority. 

That means the old problem where the mod basically woke up and declared:

> “Congratulations, Anyi, you're a cop now.” 😂



is no longer happening in this build.

Likewise, Mission Row is actually holding correctly in this session. The runtime repeatedly reports:

Profile=LSPD | Station=MissionRow

and the patrol action explicitly says:

Selected-station patrol reused existing Police vehicle | Station=MissionRow. 

So the older Del Perro persistence problem is not reproduced in this current run.

Your INI handling also looks correct now. The runtime repeatedly states:

Existing AnyiLSPDPolice.ini preserved; automatic rewrite blocked.

So your configuration isn't being silently regenerated by this build during these operations. 

There's also evidence that your Police/Citizen separation is working correctly: when Police Authority is selected, the Citizen layer explicitly reports that it is inactive. 

That is good architecture.

Now let's talk about the one thing I think still needs a real structural improvement rather than a cosmetic fix: the response/dispatch relationship.

Your Core currently has Dispatch, Convoy, Chaos, Gang Integration, Random Events, NPC Reaction, and Police Response all running in one update coordinator. That's why it works when all state transitions line up — but it's also why a stale response unit, a stale convoy terminal state, or a missing suspect can affect another subsystem.

The good news is that your current code already has some excellent protections. The Dispatch explicitly refuses to auto-complete a transport unless the incident is in a transport lifecycle, and the Core only consumes a completed convoy when the dispatch is also in the transport lifecycle.  

That's why your successful 15:19 completion did not produce the old “prisoner delivered but dispatch still active” problem.

The screenshot-style nightmare from the earlier build:

Transport Success while Dispatch still active

is no longer reproduced in this current successful path.

So I would preserve that logic.

What I would fix next is much narrower:

First: make the Dispatch→Convoy custody handoff atomic. Once arrest is secured and Convoy accepts the prisoner, Dispatch must stop owning the physical prisoner entity. Convoy becomes the sole physical owner until terminal completion. That would eliminate the instant Prisoner entity was lost during custody failure. The runtime proves this is the most concrete remaining transport defect. 

Second: release the assigned response unit as soon as custody starts. That stops the repeated “Driver sent to scene” spam during transport. 

Third: make dispatch suspect creation type-aware. Right now a generic model fallback can be used and FindGangSuspectModel() returns the first suitable external gang hash it finds. That isn't enough for believable suspect variety. The current system can indeed become biased toward a small subset of available models. 

Fourth: decouple Chaos Activity selection from nearest-location-only selection. Keep all 18 activities, but choose from a varied pool with location cooldowns and a minimum distance from the previous activity. That gives you the “across Los Santos/Blaine County” feeling you're after without needing dozens of new systems. 

Fifth: Chaos scene resolution should track actual scene entities rather than simply waiting until the area becomes non-hostile. That would make Bank Heist/Weapon Smuggling/Mass Shootout scenes feel like actual police operations instead of “combat stopped, therefore case solved.” 

And there is one especially interesting thing in your logs: the NPC civilian interaction system is actually alive in the wider runtime, not just dead code. Earlier runtime evidence shows:

POLICE_CIVILIAN_INTERACTION_SCAN → Citizen interaction STARTED → Citizen greeting phase → Citizen entered document presentation → Citizen FLED → NPC Disagree / Escalate

So that subsystem has at least been executing real interaction states in the project. 

That matters because it means we shouldn't rewrite the entire NPC system from zero either. It needs to be connected more cleanly to the Police-mode interaction command and scene ownership, rather than treated as nonexistent.

So my overall verdict for this 8/31 current build is:

Your Police Authority foundation is working.

The strongest parts right now are role separation, station persistence, Police activation, dispatch lifecycle, response creation, suspect surrender/arrest, transport state tracking, prison arrival detection, and automatic final dispatch closure. The runtime proves all of those.  

The biggest remaining weakness is not “the whole system is broken.” It's that certain physical entities and subsystem owners aren't being handed from one lifecycle to the next cleanly. The first kidnapping case exposes that brutally, while the second reckless-driver case demonstrates that the intended full chain can work.

That is actually a very useful place to be, because now I wouldn't touch the stable parts unnecessarily. I'd treat 8/31 11:45 AM as the baseline, preserve Dispatch's successful terminal handshake, preserve the startup-role/station behavior, and make the next changes surgically around custody handoff, stale response release, suspect variety, and Chaos geographic/resolution behavior.

And yeah... seeing 15:19:34 → Dispatch=None | Convoy=None | Ready for next police activity in the real runtime is satisfying. 😂🚓 That particular monster has finally been slain.

while the dispatch itself was still sitting around waiting.

Instead of simply doing:

the newer version checks whether there is actually an active incident and whether the dispatch is in the proper transport lifecycle before closing it. It also clears the convoy's terminal state after the dispatch has actually been closed. 

So the new code is not merely a smaller version of your Core. It's a transport-focused implementation with additional safeguards.

Ahhh, I see it now. 😶 This time the screenshot is actually very useful because the message on screen tells us the failure point:

> “Transport success was ignored because the dispatch is not in a custody/transport state.”



That is not a prison-arrival problem anymore. The Convoy has already reached its terminal state; the problem is the Dispatch state machine no longer agrees that the same incident is a transport/custody incident.

And yes — the previous “fix” was too dependent on the enum state. That is why it could still fail in gameplay.

Your current Convoy does correctly reach:

_state = AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Completed;

inside the prison-arrival block, then it performs cleanup and remains Completed. 

Your Core, however, deliberately does this afterward:

else if (_convoy.State == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Completed &&
         _dispatch.HasIncident)
{
    // Keep dispatch alive until explicit terminal confirmation
    ...
}

So the Convoy says:

BOOKING COMPLETED
       ↓
Convoy = Completed

but Core says:

Convoy = Completed
       ↓
DO NOT CLOSE DISPATCH YET
       ↓
wait for T

That directly conflicts with the behavior you have been asking for: successful prison booking should finish the dispatch automatically and start cooldown. 

And there is another weakness in the Dispatch code. Its CompleteTransportSuccess() does this:

if (!IsTransportLifecycleState(_incident.State))
{
    ...
    return "Transport success was ignored because the dispatch is not in a custody/transport state.";
}

So even though Convoy says Completed, Dispatch refuses the completion when its own _incident.State isn't one of the accepted lifecycle values. 

That is the real reason you're getting the annoying message.

The proper fix

I would not rewrite your Convoy.

The clean minimal correction is:

ARRESTED
   ↓
CONVOY START
   ↓
Dispatch remembers: "this incident owns a custody transport"
   ↓
Station
   ↓
Agree → PrisonTransfer
   ↓
Prison arrival
   ↓
Convoy = Completed
   ↓
Core immediately tells Dispatch:
"this exact active incident has completed custody"
   ↓
Dispatch CompleteSuccessful()
   ↓
_incident = null
yellow blip removed
waypoint restored
response unit released
dispatch cooldown starts
   ↓
next dispatch can be offered

The important change is that prison completion should be authoritative, rather than asking Dispatch to infer whether it is still in a transport state.

What I would change in your current files

In AnyiLSPDCore.cs, this part:

else if (_convoy.State == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Completed &&
         _dispatch.HasIncident)
{
    LspdResponseLog.Write(
        "POLICE_CONVOY_TICK",
        "TRANSPORT_BOOKING_READY | Dispatch retained for explicit terminal confirmation.");
}

should instead become something equivalent to:

else if (_convoy.State == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Completed &&
         _dispatch.HasIncident)
{
    string completion = _dispatch.CompleteTransportSuccess();

    LspdResponseLog.Write(
        "POLICE_CONVOY_TICK",
        "TRANSPORT_SUCCESS_TERMINAL | " + completion);

    if (!_dispatch.HasIncident)
        _convoy.ClearTerminalState();
}

That is the biggest missing connection in your current Core. Your own comments say the Convoy should automatically close the linked Dispatch, but the actual branch still intentionally keeps it alive. 

Then Dispatch needs one small correction

The current Dispatch implementation is too strict:

if (!IsTransportLifecycleState(_incident.State))
    return "Transport success was ignored...";

That guard is what produces exactly the message you're seeing. 

Instead of making prison completion depend exclusively on whatever AnyiPoliceDispatchState happens to be at that instant, Dispatch should remember that custody was established.

For example, a private flag:

private bool _transportCustodyEstablished;

When custody begins:

public void SetAwaitingTransport()
{
    if (_incident == null)
        return;

    _transportCustodyEstablished = true;
    SetState(AnyiPoliceDispatchState.AwaitingTransport);
}

And when the Convoy successfully takes over:

public void SetConvoyState(AnyiPoliceDispatchState state)
{
    if (_incident == null)
        return;

    if (state == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.AwaitingTransport ||
        state == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.PickupEnRoute ||
        state == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Escorting ||
        state == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.HoldingAtStation ||
        state == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.PrisonTransfer)
    {
        _transportCustodyEstablished = true;
    }

    if (state != AnyiPoliceDispatchState.None)
        SetState(state);
}

Then CompleteTransportSuccess() can use:

if (!_transportCustodyEstablished)
{
    // genuine protection against stale transport callbacks
}

instead of relying entirely on the current enum.

That gives you the protection we originally wanted — a stale old convoy cannot close a brand-new dispatch — without causing the legitimate prison completion to be rejected.

And after CompleteSuccessful():

_transportCustodyEstablished = false;

so the next dispatch starts cleanly.

Your station “Disagree” path has the same underlying weakness

You currently have:

public string DeclinePrisonTransfer()
{
    ...
    string result = _convoy.DeclineAtStation();

    if (_dispatch.HasIncident)
    {
        _dispatch.CompleteSuccessful(
            "Prison transfer declined. Custody closed and the justice job was completed at the station.",
            _config.DispatchSuccessAudioCategories);
    }

    return result;
}

The user-facing behavior you requested is actually correct here:

Arrest
 ↓
Station custody
 ↓
"Do you want prison transfer?"
       ↙             ↘
    NO               YES
     ↓                ↓
SUCCESS            PrisonTransfer
     ↓                ↓
Cooldown          Prison booking
                       ↓
                    SUCCESS
                       ↓
                    Cooldown

So Decline should immediately complete the dispatch at the station. We don't need to invent another mission state for it. Your Core already contains that path.

because the second system is rejecting the completion based on its own state.

can reject a legitimate completed prison transport.

public string CompleteTransportSuccess()

    if (_incident == null)
        return "No active dispatch remains.";

    CompleteSuccessful(
        "Prisoner transferred, delivered and booked successfully.",
        _config.TransportSuccessAudioCategories);

    if (!_hasIncident)
        return "Transport success. Dispatch completed and cooldown started.";

public string CompleteTransportSuccess()
{
    if (_incident == null)
        return "No active dispatch remains.";

    CompleteSuccessful(
        "Prisoner transferred, delivered and booked successfully.",
        _config.TransportSuccessAudioCategories);

    if (!HasIncident)
        return "Transport success. Dispatch completed and cooldown started.";

    return "Transport success recorded, but Dispatch is still active.";
}

So I would make CompleteTransportSuccess() slightly more direct:

public string CompleteTransportSuccess()
{
    if (_incident == null)
        return "No active dispatch remains.";

    string title = _incident.Title;
    AnyiPoliceDispatchState stateBeforeCompletion = _incident.State;

    CompleteSuccessful(
        "Prisoner transferred, delivered and booked successfully.",
        _config.TransportSuccessAudioCategories);

    if (_incident == null)
    {
        LspdResponseLog.Write(
            "POLICE_DISPATCH",
            "TRANSPORT_SUCCESS_CONFIRMED | State=" +
            stateBeforeCompletion +
            " | DispatchClosed=" +
            title);

        return "Transport success. Dispatch completed automatically.";
    }

    LspdResponseLog.Write(
        "POLICE_DISPATCH_GUARD",
        "Transport success acknowledgement did not close the dispatch | State=" +
        _incident.State +
        " | Title=" +
        title);

    return "Transport success was recorded, but the dispatch remained active.";
}

So we're not deleting your whole Dispatch system. We're simply saying:

> “Core already knows that the Convoy reached the terminal prison-booking state. Let the successful transport completion close the linked dispatch instead of having another state guard reject it.”



Your CompleteSuccessful() already performs exactly the things you want: it sets the dispatch to Completed, cleans the blips, restores the waypoint, sets _cooldownUntil, releases the response unit, and finally sets _incident = null. 

That _incident = null is the important part. That's what actually makes the dispatch no longer active.

And your Convoy's prison-arrival code already sets:

and performs its cleanup.

Ahhh, Anyi — now we have the smoking gun. 😭🕵️‍♀️ And this time I’m not guessing from the code alone; your September 1 runtime log literally shows what is happening.

The important line is:

> State=PrisonTransfer | ... | IGNORED stale transport completion



And immediately after that:

> Transport success recorded, but Dispatch is still active.



It repeats over and over. 

So your prison transport itself is actually working. The suspect reaches prison, the convoy becomes completed, and the completion code is being called. The thing failing is the last handoff from Convoy → Dispatch.

That's also why you still see the yellow dispatch blip: CompleteSuccessful() never gets to the cleanup lines because its guard rejects the completion first. Your current CompleteSuccessful() does cleanup, removes the incident, restores the waypoint, and starts _cooldownUntil, but the guard is stopping execution before those things happen. 

And I found something especially suspicious: your current Dispatch file already has PrisonTransfer inside IsTransportLifecycleState(). 

So we don't need to keep fighting that helper. We're going to make transport completion use its own small, direct completion path. Much cleaner and much less invasive.

Do this in AnyiLSPDDispatch.cs

Find your current:

public string CompleteTransportSuccess()
{
    ...
}

Delete only that method and replace it with this:

public string CompleteTransportSuccess()
{
    if (_incident == null)
        return "No active dispatch remains.";

    AnyiPoliceIncident incident = _incident;

    LspdResponseLog.Write(
        "POLICE_DISPATCH",
        "TRANSPORT_SUCCESS_BEGIN | State=" +
        incident.State +
        " | Title=" +
        incident.Title);

    // Prison/transport completion is already authorized by the Convoy
    // subsystem. The Convoy only reaches Completed after the prisoner
    // has actually arrived and booking has been completed.
    //
    // Therefore do NOT pass this through the generic stale-completion
    // guard. That guard is what was rejecting the valid PrisonTransfer
    // completion in the current runtime.
    SetState(AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Completed);

    _audio.PlayFirstAvailable(
        _config.TransportSuccessAudioCategories,
        true);

    QueueOwnedIncidentCleanup(incident);

    CleanupBlip();
    CleanupSuspectBlip();
    RestorePreviousWaypoint();

    _cooldownUntil =
        DateTime.UtcNow.AddSeconds(
            _config.DispatchCooldownSeconds);

    if (_assignedUnit != null)
        _response.ReleaseUnit(_assignedUnit);

    _assignedUnit = null;
    _deadSuspectSceneAcknowledged = false;

    _incident = null;

    if (EnableDispatchScreenNotifications)
    {
        Notification.PostTicker(
            "~g~POLICE DISPATCH SUCCESS~s~\n" +
            "Prisoner transferred, delivered and booked successfully." +
            "\n~c~Dispatch completed. Cooldown started.",
            false,
            false);
    }

    LspdResponseLog.Write(
        "POLICE_DISPATCH",
        "TRANSPORT_SUCCESS_CONFIRMED | DispatchClosed=" +
        incident.Title +
        " | CooldownStarted=true");

    return "Transport success. Dispatch completed automatically.";
}

Why this particular change?

Because right now your flow is:

Suspect arrested
       ↓
Station
       ↓
"I'll transport"
       ↓
PrisonTransfer
       ↓
Arrive at prison
       ↓
Convoy = Completed
       ↓
CompleteTransportSuccess()
       ↓
❌ generic completion guard
       ↓
Dispatch remains active
       ↓
Yellow blip remains
       ↓
No cooldown
       ↓
Cannot start next dispatch

The new method makes the final part:

Convoy = Completed
       ↓
CompleteTransportSuccess()
       ↓
Set Dispatch = Completed
       ↓
Remove yellow blip
       ↓
Remove suspect blip
       ↓
Restore waypoint
       ↓
Start cooldown
       ↓
_clear incident
       ↓
Dispatch = NONE
       ↓
Next dispatch can be offered

That's exactly what you were asking for. ❤️

And importantly, I'm not changing your whole Dispatch architecture. Your normal Complete(), CompleteSuccessful(), arrest flow, dispatch generation, audio, response units, etc. stay intact. We're only fixing the special terminal function that is currently broken.

Your existing CompleteSuccessful() can remain exactly as it is. Its stale-completion protection is still useful for preventing an old prison operation from accidentally completing a brand-new dispatch. 

Then make one tiny Core change

         _dispatch.HasIncident &&
         _dispatch.IsInTransportLifecycle)
{
    string transportCompletion = _dispatch.CompleteTransportSuccess();
    ...
}

The IsInTransportLifecycle check is another unnecessary gate for this particular terminal event.

Replace only that else if block with:

else if (_convoy.State == AnyiPoliceDispatchState.Completed &&
         _dispatch.HasIncident)

        _dispatch.CompleteTransportSuccess();

        transportCompletion);

            "POLICE_CONVOY_TICK",
            "TRANSPORT_LIFECYCLE_CLOSED | Dispatch=None | Convoy=None | Ready for next police activity.");
    }

That's it.

The Convoy's Completed state is already generated specifically when the prisoner reaches the configured prison and booking is completed. Your Convoy code explicitly sets _state = Completed, plays the transport success, cleans up, and logs "Booking complete."

Yes! 😄 Those candidates are simply the pool of possible pedestrian models that your SelectDispatchSuspectModel() method can choose from.

For example, your code currently has:

string[] candidates =
{
    "a_m_m_business_01",
    "a_m_m_skidrow_01",
    "a_m_m_hillbilly_01",
    "a_m_m_salton_01",
    "a_m_y_hipster_01",
    "a_m_y_stbla_01",
    "a_m_y_downtown_01",
    "a_f_m_tourist_01",
    "a_f_y_hipster_01"
};

Think of it like a bag of suspect models. 🎲

// Ordinary dispatches use a varied civilian/compatible gang pool.

So the intended behavior is already:

Gang-specific dispatch → use GangData → gang-associated suspect.

One more thing: simply adding 80 ped models doesn't automatically guarantee no immediate repetition. If your selection code is purely random, you can still theoretically get:

business_01 → business_01 → business_01

because random selection has no memory.

If your goal is specifically “don't give me the same suspect model repeatedly”, the better small improvement is to keep the candidate array and add a tiny last-model/recent-model exclusion to the selection method. That would be a much smaller and safer change than rewriting your dispatch system.

So yes, you're looking at the right place. candidates is exactly the place to expand the variety, while the actual selection logic determines whether that variety is genuinely randomized or repetition-controlled. 😄🚓
