September 1, 2026 — yep, Anyi 😭😂 I understand the exact direction now. This is not supposed to be another rewrite of your whole LSPD system. The goal is specifically to make Police Mode NPC interaction feel alive and police-aware, while leaving your already-working Dispatch/Convoy system alone.

I’ve handed this codebase-editing work into the new Work session so the actual uploaded project can be inspected and modified safely. The important constraint I passed along is: Ped Reaction first, minimal Core/Dispatch involvement only if absolutely necessary, no new siren/native hashes, no GangData/MemberPool changes, and preserve your current successful dispatch behavior.

And your desired behavior is clear:

Your ordinary pedestrians should not behave as if Anyi is just another GTA protagonist. During normal Police Duty, they can notice you and behave naturally, but they shouldn't all flee like you're a zombie 😂. During an active police scene, nearby civilians can become nervous, back away, leave the area, or simply observe depending on the situation. Vehicles should stop for your police interaction rather than randomly ramming Venti.

For foot interaction, the NPC should actually stop and remain engaged with Anyi instead of continuing to walk while the UI says you're talking to them 🤡. You approach → NPC notices → stops → interaction begins → NPC gives a small document/hand gesture → waits → Anyi chooses the response. A compliant citizen leaves naturally; a disobedient citizen can flee and become a pursuit.

For traffic stops, the same idea applies. You activate the existing siren normally — no new siren hash or special siren system — the selected vehicle pulls over, and only that selected vehicle is treated as the interaction target. The driver stays inside instead of magically getting out. You approach on foot, interact with the driver, they perform the document gesture, and then your decision determines whether they leave normally or flee. If they flee, the actual vehicle must physically drive away, rather than producing the hilarious "Driver Fled" notification while the poor NPC car sits there like:

> I have decided to flee spiritually. 🤡🚗



And importantly, the system needs to distinguish this from your Gang & Turf and Chaos activities. Those systems should retain their own behavior.

The biggest integration point is also understood: NPC interaction must temporarily take priority over ordinary NPC-reaction logic, so another dispatch event doesn't suddenly start while you're standing in front of a citizen conducting an investigation. Your dispatch cooldown/config should remain untouched. The Ped Reaction system should simply recognize that an interaction is currently active and avoid fighting with it.

For the police-awareness behavior, I'm also preserving the distinction you specifically wanted: not "everyone ignores Anyi." That's too lifeless. Civilians can still react. They just shouldn't constantly attack, ram, or provoke a police officer for no reason. A genuinely hostile NPC can still escalate, surrender, or flee depending on the situation.

So the intended gameplay loop becomes much more like:

Police Duty → patrol → notice suspicious pedestrian/vehicle → approach → NPC reacts naturally → interaction → document gesture → Anyi decision → citizen leaves OR complies OR flees → pursuit if necessary.

And separately:

Active Dispatch → Dispatch owns the scene → Ped Reaction supports the scene without hijacking it.

That's the important boundary. 😊

Also, I'm keeping your already-successful dispatch result as the baseline: arrest → comply → prison decision → accept transport → prison delivery → mission success → next dispatch, or decline transport → justified mission completion → cooldown → next activity. That part should not be casually rewritten just to improve NPC interaction.

One correction to your wording, naturally: “The vehicle ped I’m trying to stop is just running away, and the response message only displays ‘Driver fled,’ but the vehicle is still stopped there.” — that's exactly the bug that needs to be addressed in the traffic-interaction portion.

Your current Work-session Core contains this around the Update() method:

bool wasChaosActive = _chaosGangActivity.HasActiveActivity;

    wasChaosActive ? _chaosGangActivity.ActiveCenter : Vector3.Zero;

bool npcInteractionActive =
    _pedReaction != null &&
    _pedReaction.HasActiveInteraction;

bool canRunGangLayer =
    !_dispatch.HasIncident &&
    !npcInteractionActive;

bool gangIncidentActive = _gangIntegration.HasActiveIncident;
bool chaosActivityActive = _chaosGangActivity.HasActiveActivity;
bool sceneAreaBlocked = IsRecentSceneAreaBlocked(anyi);

Then the Gang layer should be:

if (canRunGangLayer &&
    (gangIncidentActive || !chaosActivityActive) &&
    !sceneAreaBlocked &&
    now >= _nextGangAttackCheck)
{
    _gangIntegration.Update(anyi, _nearby, _gangData, _audio);
    _nextGangAttackCheck = now.AddMilliseconds(
        _gangIntegration.HasActiveIncident
            ? 750
            : Math.Max(5000, _config.GangAttackOfferCooldownSeconds * 1000));
}

Then the Chaos layer gets one extra condition:

if (!_dispatch.HasIncident &&
    !_gangIntegration.HasActiveIncident &&
    !npcInteractionActive &&
    !sceneAreaBlocked)
{
    _chaosGangActivity.Update(anyi, _nearby, _gangData, _audio);
}

And the automatic random-dispatch discovery gets the same condition:

sceneAreaBlocked = IsRecentSceneAreaBlocked(anyi);

if (!_dispatch.HasIncident &&
    !_gangIntegration.HasActiveIncident &&
    !_chaosGangActivity.HasActiveActivity &&
    !npcInteractionActive &&
    !sceneAreaBlocked)
{
    AnyiPoliceIncident discovered =
        _randomEvents.TryDiscover(anyi, _nearby, _gangData);

    if (discovered != null)
        _dispatch.Offer(discovered);
}
